Art. 140: If the person referred to in Article 136 is unable to provide the requested guarantee and this situation is not attributable to them, the jurisdiction concerned substitutes for said guarantee, for a duration equal to that set for the undertaking, one or more of the measures provided for in Article 134 of this code.

###### Article 144 , :The offenses enumerated in the preceding article direct part in hostilities;

are punishable, by reason of the gravity of the offense and the 3) the act of launching or directing an indiscriminate attack personal situation of their perpetrator, by a sentence of affecting a civilian population or civilian objects, knowing criminal imprisonment of thirty (30) to fifty (50) years and a that this attack will incidentally cause loss of human life, fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million injuries (100,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Section 2 :War crimes

###### Art. 145 : Constitutes a war crime, any one of the following serious violations, committed during an international armed conflict, when they target persons or property protected by the Geneva Conventions:

1) intentional homicide;

2) torture or inhuman treatment, including biological experiments;

3) intentionally causing great suffering or seriously injuring physical integrity or health;

4) destruction and appropriation of property, not justified by military necessities and carried out on a large scale in an unlawful and arbitrary manner;

5) compelling a prisoner of war or other protected person to serve in the forces of a hostile power;

6) intentionally depriving a prisoner of war or any other protected person of his or her right to be judged regularly and impartially;

7) unlawful deportation or transfer or unlawful detention;

8) taking of hostages;

Also constitute war crimes the following serious violations of the laws and customs applicable to international armed conflicts within the established framework of international law, namely any of the following acts:

1) intentionally launching deliberate attacks against the civilian population as such or against individual civilians not taking direct part in hostilities;

2) intentionally launching attacks against civilian objects, that is, objects which are not military objectives;

3) intentionally launching an attack in the knowledge that such attack will cause incidental loss of life or injury to civilians or damage to civilian objects or widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment which would be clearly excessive in relation to the concrete and direct overall military advantage anticipated;

4) intentionally directing deliberate attacks against property which are not military objectives;

5) launching an attack against works or installations containing dangerous forces, notably dams, dikes, nuclear power stations, knowing that such attack causes loss of human life, injuries to civilians or damage to civilian objects which are excessive;

6) attacking or bombarding, by whatever means, undefended localities and demilitarized zones, towns, villages, dwellings or buildings which are not defended and which therefore do not constitute military objectives;

7) killing or wounding a combatant who has laid down arms, no longer has means of defence or has surrendered;

8) causing loss of human life or serious injuries by improperly using the parliamentary flag, the flag or military insignia and uniform of the enemy, of the United Nations Organization, of neutral States or of other States not parties to the conflict, as well as the distinctive signs provided for by the Geneva Conventions and their third additional protocol;

24 Novembre 2015 JOURNAL OFFICIEL DE LAREPUBLIQUE TOGOLAISE 17

9) the transfer, directly or indirectly, by the occupying power of parts of its own civilian population into the territory it occupies, or the deportation or transfer of all or parts of the population of the occupied territory within or outside this territory, unless, in the case of a transfer within the occupied territory, the security of civilians or military imperatives so require;

10) unjustifiably delaying the repatriation of prisoners of war or civilians;

11) directing attacks against historic monuments, works of art or places of worship clearly recognized as constituting the cultural or spiritual heritage of peoples and to which special protection has been accorded, provided that such property is not located in the immediate vicinity of military objectives, nor used by the adverse party in support of its military effort;

12) outrages upon personal dignity, in particular subjecting persons to inhuman and degrading practices based on racial, ethnic, sexual discrimination;

13) depriving a person protected by the Geneva Conventions and Protocol I of his or her right to be judged regularly and impartially;

14) conscripting or enlisting children under 18 years of age into the national armed forces or into armed groups or using them to participate actively in hostilities;

15) intentionally starving civilians as a method of warfare, by depriving them of objects indispensable to their survival, including intentionally impeding relief supplies as provided for under the Geneva Conventions;

16) imposing rape, sexual slavery, prostitution, forced pregnancy, forced sterilization or any other form of sexual violence;

17) utilizing the presence of a civilian or other protected person to render certain points, areas or military forces immune from military operations;

18) employing asphyxiating, toxic or similar gases, and all analogous liquids, materials or substances;

19) employing poison or poisoned weapons;

20) pillaging a town or place, even when taken by assault;

21) declaring extinguished, suspended or inadmissible in court the rights and actions of nationals of the adverse party;

22) declaring that no quarter will be given;

23) killing or wounding by treachery individuals belonging to the hostile nation or army;

24) destroying or seizing the enemy's property, unless such destruction or seizure be imperatively demanded by the necessities of war;

25) compelling the nationals of the adverse party to take part in the operations of war directed against their own country, even if they were in the service of the belligerent before the commencement of the war;

26) employing bullets which expand or flatten easily in the human body, such as bullets with a hard envelope which does not entirely cover the core or is pierced with incisions;

27) employing weapons, projectiles and material and methods of combat of a nature to cause superfluous injury or unnecessary suffering or to strike indiscriminately in violation of the law of armed conflict, on condition that such weapons, projectiles, material and methods of combat are the subject of a comprehensive prohibition;

28) launching deliberate attacks against buildings, material, medical units and means of transport and personnel using, in conformity with international law, the distinctive signs provided for by the Geneva Conventions;

29) launching deliberate attacks against buildings dedicated to religion, education, art, science or charitable purposes, historic monuments, hospitals or places where the sick and wounded are collected, provided that such buildings are not military objectives;

30) launching deliberate attacks against personnel, installations, material, units or vehicles employed in a humanitarian assistance or peacekeeping mission in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, as long as they are entitled to the protection given to civilians and civilian objects under international law of armed conflict.

Art. 146: Constitute, furthermore, war crimes the serious violations of article 3 common to the Geneva Conventions and other serious violations of the laws and customs applicable to non-international armed conflicts, namely any of the following acts:

18 JOURNAL OFFICIEL DE LA REPUBLIQUE TOGOLAISE 24 Novembre 2015

1) violence to life and person, in particular murder of all kinds, mutilations, cruel, inhuman and degrading treatment, and torture;

2) outrages upon personal dignity, in particular humiliating and degrading treatment;

3) taking of hostages;

4) the passing of sentences and the carrying out of executions without previous judgment pronounced by a regularly constituted court, affording all judicial guarantees which are generally recognized as indispensable;

5) intentionally directing deliberate attacks against the civilian population as such or against civilians not taking direct part in hostilities;

6) intentionally directing deliberate attacks against buildings, material, medical units and means of transport and personnel using, in conformity with international law, the distinctive signs of the Geneva Conventions;

7) intentionally directing deliberate attacks against personnel, installations, material, units or vehicles employed in a humanitarian assistance or peacekeeping mission in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations as long as they are entitled to the protection given to civilians and civilian objects under international law of armed conflict;

8) intentionally directing deliberate attacks against buildings dedicated to religion, education, art, science or charitable purposes, historic monuments, hospitals and places where the sick and wounded are collected, provided that such buildings are not military objectives;

9) pillaging a town or locality, even when taken by assault;

10) rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization, or any other form of sexual violence also constituting a serious violation of article 3 common to the four Geneva Conventions;

11) conscripting or enlisting children under 18 years of age into armed forces or groups or using them to participate actively in hostilities;

12) ordering the displacement of the civilian population for reasons related to the conflict, unless the security of the civilians involved or imperative military reasons so demand;

13) killing, wounding or capturing an adversary by treachery;

14) declaring that no quarter will be given;

15) subjecting persons of another party to the conflict who have fallen into his power to physical mutilations or to medical or scientific experiments of any kind which are neither justified by the medical, dental or hospital treatment of the person concerned nor carried out in his or her interest, and which cause death to or seriously endanger the health of such person or persons;

16) destroying or seizing the property of an adversary, unless such destruction or seizure be imperatively demanded by the necessities of the conflict;

17) employing poison or poisoned weapons;

18) employing asphyxiating, toxic or similar gases, and all analogous liquids, materials or devices;

19) employing bullets which expand or flatten easily in the human body, such as bullets with a hard envelope which does not entirely cover the core or is pierced with incisions.

Art. 148: War crimes are punished, by reason of their gravity and the personal situation of their perpetrator, with imprisonment of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

Section 3: Crimes against humanity

Art. 149: Constitutes a crime against humanity, in time of peace or in time of war, any one of the following acts, committed knowingly within the framework of a widespread or systematic attack launched against any civilian population or a disarmed population in case of internal conflict:

1) murder;

2) extermination;

3) enslavement;

4) deportation or forcible transfer of population;

5) imprisonment or other severe deprivation of physical liberty in violation of fundamental rules of international law;

6) torture;

24 Novembre 2015 JOURNAL OFFICIEL DE LA REPUBLIQUE TOGOLAISE 19

7) rape, sexual slavery, enforced prostitution, forced pregnancy, enforced sterilization and any other form of sexual violence of comparable gravity;

8) persecution of any group or any identifiable collectivity on political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural, religious or gender grounds, or on other grounds universally recognized as inadmissible in international law, in connection with any act referred to in this article;

9) enforced disappearances of persons;

10) crime of apartheid;

11) other inhumane acts of a similar character intentionally causing great suffering or serious injury to physical integrity or to physical or mental health.

Art. 150: For the purposes of the preceding article, the following is understood:

1) "attack launched against any civilian population or a disarmed population in case of conflict" means a course of conduct involving the multiple commission of acts referred to in the preceding article against any civilian population, pursuant to or in furtherance of a State or organizational policy to commit such attack;

2) "extermination" includes the intentional infliction of conditions of life, inter alia the deprivation of access to food and medicine, calculated to bring about the destruction of part of a population;

3) "enslavement" means the exercise of any or all of the powers attaching to the right of ownership over a person, including in the course of trafficking in human beings, in particular women and children;

4) "deportation or forcible transfer of population" means forced displacement of the persons concerned by expulsion or other coercive acts from the area in which they are lawfully present, without grounds permitted under international law;

5) "sexual slavery" means the use of a person as a sexual object;

6) "torture" means the intentional infliction of severe pain or suffering, whether physical or mental, upon a person in the custody or under the control of the accused. The term does not include pain or suffering arising only from, inherent in or incidental to, lawful sanctions;

7) "forced pregnancy" means the unlawful confinement of a woman forcibly made pregnant, with the intent of affecting the ethnic composition of any population or of carrying out other grave violations of international law;

8) "persecution" means the intentional and severe deprivation of fundamental rights contrary to international law by reason of the identity of the group or collectivity concerned;

9) "crime of apartheid" means inhumane acts of a character similar to those referred to in the present article committed in the context of an institutionalized regime of systematic oppression and domination by one racial group over any other racial group or groups and committed with the intention of maintaining that regime;

10) "enforced disappearances of persons" means the arrest, detention or abduction of persons by, or with the authorization, support or acquiescence of, a State or a political organization, followed by a refusal to acknowledge that deprivation of freedom or to give information on the fate or whereabouts of those persons, with the intention of removing them from the protection of the law for a prolonged period of time.

Art. 151: Crimes against humanity are punished, by reason of their gravity and the personal situation of their perpetrator, with imprisonment of thirty (30) to fifty (50) years and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

Section 4: Crime of apartheid

Art. 152: Apartheid is any act committed with a view to instituting or maintaining the domination of one racial group of human beings over any other racial group of human beings for the purpose of systematically oppressing them.

Art. 153: Constitute the crime of apartheid, as a crime against humanity, any one of the following inhumane acts:

1) denial of life of members of a racial group or several racial groups;

2) serious impairment to the physical or mental integrity, liberty or dignity of the members of a racial group or several racial groups, or subjecting them to torture or to cruel, inhuman or degrading punishment and treatment;

3) arbitrary arrest and unlawful imprisonment of members of a racial group or several racial groups;

20 JOURNAL OFFICIEL DE LA REPUBLIQUE TOGOLAISE 24 Novembre 2015

4) intentionally inflicting on a racial group or several racial groups living conditions calculated to bring about its or their physical destruction in whole or in part;

5) adopting legislative or other measures, intended to prevent a racial group or several racial groups from participation in the political, social, economic and cultural life of the country;

6) deliberately creating conditions preventing the development of the group or groups considered, in particular by depriving the members of a racial group or of several racial groups of fundamental freedoms and rights of man: the right to work, the right to form recognized trade unions, the right to education, the right to a nationality, the right to freedom of movement and residence, the right to freedom of opinion and of expression, and the right to freedom of peaceful assembly and association;

7) adopting measures, including legislative measures, designed to divide the population along racial lines by the creation of separate reserves and ghettos for the members of a racial group or of several racial groups, by the prohibition of mixed marriages among members of various racial groups, and by the expropriation of landed property and goods belonging to a racial group or to several racial groups or to members of such groups;

8) exploiting the labour of the members of a racial group or of several racial groups, in particular by submitting them to forced labour;

9) persecuting organizations or persons, by depriving them of fundamental freedoms and rights, because they oppose apartheid.

Art. 154: Crimes of apartheid are punished, by reason of their gravity and the personal situation of their perpetrator, with imprisonment of thirty (30) to fifty (50) years and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

Section 5: Provisions common to the crime of genocide, war crimes, crimes against humanity and the crime of apartheid

Art. 155: The official capacity of perpetrator of crimes of genocide, war crimes, crimes against humanity and apartheid, as Head of State or Government, as elected representative or as agent of the State, does not exempt him from his criminal responsibility under this code. It may not constitute, in any case, a ground for reduction, suppression or suspension of sentence.

Art. 157: Criminally and individually responsible for the crimes referred to in this chapter and liable to the penalties provided for this purpose, are representatives of the State, including military chiefs and other hierarchical superiors, members of organizations and institutions, as well as individuals or groups participating therein as perpetrators or accomplices, or who are guilty of direct incitement to the perpetration of any one of these crimes, or who participate in a conspiracy to commit it.

Any military commander and other hierarchical superior are criminally responsible for crimes committed by forces or subordinates placed under their authority and effective control if:

1) they knew or, in view of the circumstances, should have known that the crime was going to or was being committed or deliberately disregarded information which clearly indicated it;

2) they had not taken all necessary and reasonable measures within their power to prevent or repress its execution or to refer the matter to the competent authorities for the purposes of investigation and prosecution;

3) the crime was related to activities falling within their responsibility and effective control.

Art. 158: Natural persons who are perpetrators or accomplices of the crimes referred to in this chapter incur, in addition, the following penalties:

1) general confiscation according to the terms set out in articles 69 and following of this code as well as confiscation of profits, property and assets derived directly or indirectly from the crime, without prejudice to the rights of third parties in good faith;

###### Art. 155: The provisions of this chapter apply to crimes committed on national territory or outside it, whatever the nationality of the perpetrator or accomplice and the place of commission of the offense.

2) civil disqualification according to the procedures set out in Article 72 of this code;

3) prohibition of residence according to the procedures set out in Articles 113 et seq. of this code;

4) special confiscation according to the procedures set out in Articles 117 et seq. of this code.

###### Art. 156:

The provisions of this chapter apply to all equally without any distinction.

The court shall, in addition, impose a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to one hundred million (100,000,000) CFA francs.

24 Novembre 2015 JOURNAL OFFICIEL DE LA REPUBLIQUE TOGOLAISE 21

###### Art. 159: The penalties incurred by legal persons declared criminally liable for the crimes provided for in this chapter are:

1) the penalties provided for in Article 54 of this code;

2) disqualification for up to ten (10) years from the right to administer or manage a company by the directors and managers who were the instigators or perpetrators of the offense committed by the company, by way of derogation from Article 55 of this code;

3) publication of the conviction in the official journal and in the press according to the procedures provided for in Article 142 of this code.

The court shall, in addition, impose a fine of one hundred million (100,000,000) to five hundred million (500,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 160: A person responsible for one of the crimes referred to in this chapter cannot be exonerated from their liability solely on the grounds that they performed an act prescribed or authorized by legislative and regulatory provisions or an act authorized by a legitimate authority.

An order from a legitimate authority does not exonerate a person from their liability for the crimes provided for in this chapter, except if:

1) that person had a legal obligation to obey the orders of the government or superior in question;

2) that person did not know that the order was illegal; and

3) the order was not manifestly illegal.

These three conditions are cumulative.

For the purposes of this article, an order to commit genocide or a crime against humanity is deemed manifestly illegal.

###### Art. 161: Also constitute grounds for exoneration from criminal liability:

1) an illness or mental deficiency depriving a person of the ability to understand the unlawful nature or character of their conduct or to control such conduct so as to conform it to the requirements of law;

2) a state of intoxication, except voluntary intoxication in circumstances where the person knew that as a result of their intoxication, they risked adopting conduct constituting a crime or took no account of that risk;

3) legitimate self-defense of oneself or others, or in the case of war crimes, to defend property essential to their survival or that of others or essential to the accomplishment of a military mission, against imminent and unlawful use of force, provided the principle of proportionality is respected;

4) cases of necessity and duress resulting from a threat of imminent death or serious, continuing or imminent harm to their own physical integrity or that of others, provided they act reasonably and do not intend to cause greater harm than that to be avoided.

###### Art. 162: The fact that a person participated in a defensive operation conducted by armed forces does not in itself constitute a ground for exoneration from criminal liability within the meaning of the preceding article.

###### Art. 163: In the event of concurrent offenses involving several categories of crimes, the penalty corresponding to the most serious offense shall apply.

However, the ancillary penalties relating thereto may, in certain cases, be combined with the principal penalty if the duration of the latter does not exceed thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 164: The crime of genocide, war crimes, crimes against humanity, and crimes of apartheid are not subject to any statute of limitations.

Chapitre II: Des atteintes à la vie de la personne

Section 1.: Des homicides volontaires

###### Paragraphe 1: Du meurtre et de l'assassinat

###### Art. 165: Murder is the act of intentionally causing the death of another person.

It is punishable by a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 166: Murder is punishable by twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment:

1) when it precedes, accompanies or follows another crime;

2) when its purpose is to prepare, facilitate or execute an offense;

3) when its purpose is to facilitate the escape or ensure the impunity of the perpetrator or accomplice;

4) when it was committed for ritual purposes or cannibalism;

22 JOURNAL OFFICIEL DE LA REPUBLIQUE TOGOLAISE 24 Novembre 2015

5) when it was committed in response to conduct perceived as having harmed the honor of the family.

###### Art. 167: Assassination is murder committed with premeditation or by ambush.

Premeditation is the deliberate intent, formed before the act, to commit a specific crime or offense.

Ambush consists of waiting for an individual in a place either to kill them or to commit acts of violence against them.

###### Art. 168: Any person who commits assassination is punishable by twenty (20) to fifty (50) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 169: Parricide is murder committed by a descendant upon the person of their biological or adoptive father or mother or the ascendants of the latter.

###### Art. 170: Any person who commits parricide is punishable by twenty (20) to thirty (30) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 171: Infanticide is the murder of a child under the age of eighteen (18) years.

###### Art. 172: Infanticide is punishable by ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

Paragraphe 2: De l'empoisonnement et de l'administration de substances nuisibles à la santé

###### Art. 176: Is guilty of the act of intentional transmission of the human immunodeficiency virus (HIV), a person who:

1) knowing they are infected, intentionally, by whatever means, transmits HIV to another person;

2) knowing that the objects they use are contaminated by HIV, uses said objects on natural persons;

3) intentionally injects substances infected by HIV into another person;

4) knowing that the blood offered for transfusion, tissue or organ given to be transplanted is infected by HIV, permits its use or proceeds with a blood transfusion or tissue or organ transplant on a person.

A person infected by HIV may benefit from mitigating circumstances for an act of intentional transmission under this chapter if:

1) they took all known useful measures to substantially reduce the risk of contamination, particularly by using a condom; and

2) informed their sexual partner of their HIV-positive status and obtained their free and informed consent prior to any sexual relations.

###### Section 1:Of torture and other ill-treatment

###### Art. 198 :The term «torture» designates any act by which lawyer, an agent or officer of the security or defense forces or

###### Art.337; Also constitutes an offense under Articles 336

and 337 trafficking in organs, tissues, human blood or Any person who uses or exploits a particularly vulnerable its derivatives, the use of all or part of the human body person, particularly due to their minority, advanced age, for the purpose of any mystical or ritual practice whatsoever pregnancy, illness, infirmity or physical or mental deficiency, for the purposes referred to in the preceding paragraph, is punishable by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

Any person guilty of trafficking in organs, tissues, human blood or its derivatives for the purpose of any mystical or ritual practice whatsoever is punishable by a sentence of criminal imprisonment of twenty (20) to thirty (30) years and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs.

The sentence may be increased up to twenty (20) years imprisonment if there has been use of violence.

###### Section 5: On exploitation Art. 341: Forced or servile marriage is any institution or practice whereby:

###### Paragraph 1

On the exploitation of the human person

1) an adult person, without their consent, or a child, is promised or given in marriage in return for financial or material consideration;

Art. 338: Any person, whatever means are used, who commits the offense of forced labor or services, is punishable by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

2) a person is transferred to a third party, for consideration or not, by their spouse or a member of their family or clan with a view to marriage;

3) a person is abducted or held against their will with a view to marriage.

Any person who practices, facilitates or derives financial or material profit from the enslavement of others or a similar practice.

Any person who subjects another to forced or servile marriage as defined by Article 150 of this code, is liable to a sentence of ten (10) to twenty (20) years imprisonment and a fine of ten million (10,000,000) to thirty million (30,000,000) CFA francs.

is liable to a sentence of three (03) years to five (5) years imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 339: Any person who exploits another person, by force, through pornography is liable to a sentence of five (05) years to ten (10) years criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

Art. 342: The exploitation of a child in armed conflict, the act by any person of enrolling a child in armed forces, incorporating them into an armed group or having them take part in hostilities, is prohibited and constitutes exploitation.

The preceding paragraph applies both to child victims of international armed conflicts and to child victims of internal armed conflicts, internal tensions and civil unrest. It also applies to children who, before the start of hostilities, are considered refugees within the meaning of relevant international law or legislative provisions in force.

Any person who uses or exploits a particularly vulnerable person, particularly due to their minority, advanced age, state of pregnancy, illness, infirmity or physical or mental deficiency, for pornographic purposes, is punishable by a sentence of imprisonment of ten (10) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

Any person who engages in the exploitation of a child in armed conflict is punishable by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

The sentence may be increased up to twenty (20) years imprisonment if there has been use of violence.

###### Art. 343: Any person who derives financial or material profit from the repeated, continuous or intermittent commission of offenses under this paragraph by a third party is punishable by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

1) the victim is under fifteen (15) years of age at the time of commission of the acts;

2) the act was committed with violence, acts of torture or barbarity;

3) the offense is committed by an organized gang;

4) in case of recidivism;

5) the offense resulted in the death of the victim or a third party, including death by suicide, or the contraction by the victim of a fatal disease or HIV/AIDS.

###### Art. 344: The offenses referred to in Articles 339 to 343 are punishable by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs, if they are accompanied by any of the following circumstances:

###### Paragraph 2: On the exploitation of begging

###### Art. 345: The exploitation of begging is the act by any person in any manner whatsoever:

1) the offense is committed against a particularly vulnerable victim, particularly due to their minority, advanced age, state of pregnancy, illness, infirmity or physical or mental deficiency;

1) of organizing the begging of others with a view to profiting therefrom;

2) of profiting from the begging of others, sharing the proceeds thereof or receiving subsidies from a person habitually engaged in begging;

2) the offense is committed against several victims;

3) of hiring, training or diverting a person with a view to having them engage in begging, or exerting pressure on them to beg or continue doing so;

3) the offense resulted in serious injury to the victim or a third party;

4) of hiring, training or diverting for purposes of personal enrichment a person with a view to having them provide a service in return for a donation on the public highway.

4) the offense is committed within the framework of activities of a criminal group;

Art. 347: Assimilated to the exploitation of begging is the fact of being unable to justify resources corresponding to one's lifestyle, while exercising a permanent or non-permanent influence over one or more persons engaged in begging or being in habitual contact with such person(s).

5) the victim of the offense was chosen by the perpetrator by reason of their nationality, ethnic origin, skin color, gender, religion or beliefs, or political opinions;

6) narcotics or psychotropic substances, medicines or weapons are used for the commission of the offense;

7) the perpetrator of the offense is in a state of recidivism;

8) the perpetrator of the offense is a civil servant;

9) the perpetrator of the offense is the spouse or cohabitant of the victim;

Art. 348: Any person guilty of exploitation of the begging of others is punishable by a sentence of imprisonment of six (06) months to three (03) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two sentences.

10) the perpetrator of the offense is in a position of authority, responsibility or trust in relation to the victim;

11) the victim was brought into contact with the perpetrator of the acts through the use, for the dissemination of messages to a wide

###### Art. 360

Constitutes a violation of the secrecy of correspondence the act for a person to order, commit or facilitate, outside the cases provided for by law, the interruption, destruction, opening, suppression, diversion, and, by extension, to subject them to other treatments such as fraudulent delays, abusive returns, intentionally erroneous distributions, even momentarily, of things entrusted to the post and, on the occasion of the exercise of which the offense was committed, or one of these two penalties.

The deprivation of rights provided for in Articles 80 and following, including the prohibition from practicing the profession in the exercise of which the offense was committed, may be pronounced as a complementary measure.

If the author, instigator, principal or accomplice of the slanderous denunciation acted by misappropriation or abuse

Also constitutes a violation of the secrecy of correspondence the act for any person to reveal the content of such correspondence.

of powers attached to his functions, he is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) years and a fine of twenty million (20,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Art. X3li: Any person who violates the secrecy of correspondence is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

If the denounced act is subject to criminal or disciplinary sanction, proceedings may be instituted pursuant to this article either after a judgment or order of discharge or acquittal, or after an order or ruling of dismissal, or after the denunciation has been dismissed by the magistrate.

The provisions of the preceding paragraph do not apply to persons:

The official, superior authority or employer competent to give it the follow-up it is likely to have.

1) who have obtained express or tacit authorization, either from the author of the correspondence, or from the person to whom its author destines it, to open the correspondence and reveal its content;

The court seized pursuant to this article must rule on whether proceedings concerning the denounced act are pending.

2) who open a correspondence and reveal its content following an authorization granted in the context of a judicial investigation by the public prosecutor or by an investigating judge.

Section 3: Violations of privacy

Paragraph 1: Violation of domicile

Art. 362: Any violation of a correspondence entrusted to the post, committed or facilitated by an agent or employee of the administration, is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, if it is established that the author has diverted or otherwise used in the exercise of his functions.

Art. 365: Constitutes a violation of domicile, the act for a person to enter a private place inhabited or serving as a dwelling, against the will or without the consent of the occupant, or to introduce oneself there or to remain there.

Also constitutes a violation of domicile, the act for a depositary of public authority or in charge of a public mission, acting in the exercise of his functions, to enter or attempt to enter into the domicile of an individual against the will of the latter outside the cases provided for by law.

24 November 2015 45

Art. 366: Any person who commits a violation of domicile is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Art. 371: Any natural or legal person authorized to participate in the execution of a telecommunications or electronic communications service, who intercepts a private communication, or who, outside the cases provided for by law, discloses, publishes or uses the content of said correspondence is punished in accordance with the provisions of the preceding article.

Art. 367: The penalty is one (01) to three (03) years imprisonment and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to three million (3,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties if the violation of domicile was committed:

The provisions of the preceding paragraph do not apply to persons:

1) in a group;

1) who have obtained express or tacit consent, either from the author of the private communication, or from the person to whom its author destines it, to the interception of the private communication and to the revelation of its content;

2) during the night;

3) by means of violence, threats, assault or coercion;

2) who intercept a private communication in accordance with an authorization issued in the context of a judicial investigation by the public prosecutor or by an investigating judge;

4) while wearing a visible or hidden weapon;

5) with the use of a false name, a false title or a false order from the legitimate authority.

3) who provide the public with a telecommunications or electronic communications service and who intercept a private communication when such interception is necessary to protect their rights or property directly related to the provision of the telecommunications or electronic communications service;

Prosecution may only be exercised upon complaint by the victim.

4) members of the personnel of the regulatory authority responsible for the management and control of the radioelectric frequency spectrum for a private communication that they intercepted in order to identify, isolate or prevent the unauthorized use of a frequency or transmission.

###### Paragraphe 2

Violation of the privacy of the person

Art. 372: Any natural or legal person who, without concurring in the cases provided for by law, in the execution of an interception decision, reveals the existence or content of the judicial or security interception is punished by the penalties provided for in Article 371.

###### Art. 368: Constitutes a violation of the privacy of a person, the act:

1) to publish or broadcast private papers or recordings, a drawing, a photograph, a film or any other medium reproducing the image of that person without his consent or that of his successors in title;

Art. 373: Any legal person operating a telecommunications or electronic communications network, or providing a telecommunications or electronic communications service, cryptology and hosting services and not complying with requisitions emanating from authorized authorities provided for by law is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of twenty-five million (25,000,000) to two

2) to organize, by whatever means, the interception, listening or recording of private communications, or oral, optical, magnetic or other exchanges received in a private place, without the knowledge or consent of the persons in communication or of the owner of the premises.

###### Art. 369: Any natural or legal person who violates the privacy of another person, is punished by a sentence of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

Chapter II: Of the manufacture and illicit trafficking in firearms, their parts, components and ammunition

Is punished by the same penalties, any person who, by the means set forth in the first paragraph, has glorified the crimes referred to in the first paragraph, war crimes, crimes against humanity or crimes of apartheid.

###### Art. 555: For the purposes of this article:

All seditious cries and songs uttered in public places or meetings are punished by one (01) to two (02) months of imprisonment or ten (10) days to sixty (60) days of community service and a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

1) the expression "firearms" means any portable barreled weapon that propels shot, a bullet or a projectile by the action of an explosive or is designed to do so or can be easily converted for that purpose, excluding antique firearms or their replicas;

2) the expression "parts and components" means any element or replacement element specifically designed for a firearm essential to its operation, including the barrel, the frame or receiver, the slide or cylinder, the breechblock or bolt, as well as any device designed or adapted to reduce the noise caused by firing a firearm;

3) the term "ammunition" means the complete cartridge or its components, including cartridge cases, primers, powder

###### Paragraphe 2: On abuse of functions

Constitutes an abuse of functions, the act by a public agent in the exercise of their functions, of a public establishment, of a State company or a mixed economy company with majority State participation, charged with functions of administration, supervision or control, who has taken interests in the enterprise, subsequently to taking up these functions or before a period of two years after their termination, to intentionally perform or refrain from performing an act in violation of laws or regulations in order to obtain an undue advantage for themselves or for a third party.

Art. 618: Any person capable of abuse of functions of a public establishment, of a State company or a mixed economy company with majority State participation, in a public administration or in a public contract or market or any public entity, to encourage or permit, either directly or indirectly, the false statements or invoices by the tenderer or supplier, in order to derive any profit therefrom, is punished by imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

###### Art. 618

Any person guilty of the acts provided for in the preceding article is punished by a penalty of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 619

The court seized may pronounce, where appropriate, against the convicted person the following complementary penalties:

1) the confiscation of sums, objects or values irregularly acquired or held. If the thing has not been seized or cannot be produced, the confiscation is ordered by value;

2) the posting or dissemination of the decision pronounced at the expense of the convicted person;

3) the prohibition to stay in Togo for a period not exceeding ten (10) years after the execution of the sentence, if the convicted person is a foreigner.

###### Paragraphe 4: On illicit enrichment

###### Art. 620: Is considered as illicit enrichment,

any acquisition of movable or immovable property by a public agent or a person depositary of public authority, which he or she cannot justify by their income declared to the tax administration or by their legitimately acquired and duly certified assets.

###### Art. 621: Any person guilty of illicit enrichment

is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine corresponding to double the value judged excessive in relation to the value of the property that the accused is likely to possess.

##### Chapter VI: Offences against the national economy

and public equipment

###### Section 1

Attacks on the national economy

Paragraph 1: Speculation and attacks on market transparency

###### Art. 622: Is punished by a penalty of imprisonment of six

(06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, any person who:

1) facilitates or participates in any sale of bonds of the Togolese State of which the seller is not the owner at the time of the conclusion of the sale when on that date, the seller has not taken the necessary arrangements for the availability of the bonds on the delivery date;

2) facilitates or participates in any derivative contract under the terms of which one of the parties pays the other a premium in exchange for a payment or other service in the event of a credit event or default affecting the Togolese State.

Art. 623: Is punished by imprisonment of three (03) months to five (05) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs anyone who, with the aim of influencing or distorting the normal course of the market, of a security, a financial instrument, a good or a title representing a good or a set of goods admitted to trading on a stock exchange or an equivalent organization:

1) knowingly disseminates slanderous, false or misleading information;

2) carries out directly or indirectly abnormal operations on these goods, values or securities; in order to obtain or attempt to obtain an economic advantage for themselves or for a third party.

Is punished by imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs, anyone who, as a member of a management, supervisory or control body of an issuing company, or who, by profession or function, has access to precise confidential information likely to have an influence on the normal course of a market, a security, a financial instrument, a good or a title representing a good or a set of goods admitted to trading on a stock exchange or an equivalent organization, obtains or attempts to obtain for themselves or for a third party an economic advantage by knowingly using this information before the public becomes aware of it:

1) by exploiting them directly or indirectly by disposing through acquisition, transfer, exchange or any other means, of the aforementioned goods, values and securities;

2) by disclosing them to anyone with or without consideration;

3) by exploiting them through the recommendation to a third party of the purchase or sale of these goods, values and securities.

Any person who exploits confidential information that they obtained or that they know was obtained through the commission of a crime or an offence is punished by the same penalties as those prescribed in the preceding paragraph.

Any person who obtains or attempts to obtain for themselves or for a third party a pecuniary advantage by exploiting confidential information is punished by imprisonment of two (02) to four (04) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Paragraphe 2

Obstructions to freedom of work in relations with third parties, intended to allow the safeguarding of their confidential character.

###### Art. 624: Constitutes an obstruction to freedom of work any

act tending to create an obstacle, in a concerted manner, to the free exercise of work.

###### Art. 625: Any person who obstructs freedom of work is

punished by a penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties. The penalties provided for in the preceding paragraph are doubled if the obstruction to freedom of work was done by means of violence, acts of force, false news, threats or fraudulent maneuvers.

Subject to international treaties or agreements and laws and regulations in force, it is prohibited, under penalty of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs, for any legal or natural person, to request, search for or communicate, in writing, orally or in any other form, in order to constitute evidence for judicial or administrative proceedings abroad or in the context thereof:

1) documents or information of a nature

###### Art. 626

Any employer who organizes the poaching for their benefit of employees of competing enterprises, by fraudulent maneuvers, is punished by a term of imprisonment of two (02) months to one (01) year and a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

economic, commercial, industrial, financial, scientific, technical or strategic information whose communication is likely to undermine sovereignty, security, the essential economic interests of Togo or public order;

2) documents or information of an economic, commercial, industrial, financial, scientific, technical or strategic nature not having a

###### Art. 627: Any person who participates in a concerted

work stoppage practiced outside the regulations in force is punished by a term of imprisonment of five (05) to fifteen (15) days or one (01) to twenty (20) day(s) of community service or a fine of two thousand (2,000) to thirty thousand (30,000) CFA francs.

public character whose disclosure would be likely to seriously compromise the interests of an enterprise, by

undermining its scientific and technical potential, its strategic positions, its commercial or financial interests or its competitive capacity or which have been the subject as such of a classification and specific protection.

The organizers of the concerted work stoppage are punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to six (06) months.

###### Paragraph 4: Fraud and violation of rules regarding investment

###### Paragraph 3: Violation of business secrecy of the enterprise and protection of the interests of the national economy

###### Art. 629: Without prejudice to administrative sanctions provided for by the investment code, any fraud or

###### Art. 628: Any person who discloses to a person not

authorized to have knowledge of it, other than Togolese judicial or administrative authorities duly authorized, information falling under business secrecy of an enterprise is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) years, and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

any serious and repeated violation of the obligations and commitments made by the investor in accordance with the regulations in force is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years, a fine of five million (5,000,000) to fifty million (50,000,000) CFA francs, or one of these two penalties.

###### Section 2: Offenses against public infrastructure

Constitute protected strategic information falling under business secrecy under this code, any element, whatever the medium, of a commercial, financial, scientific, technical, technological or strategic nature:

###### Art. 630: Is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties, any person who:

1) whose knowledge or disclosure would be likely to harm the interest of the enterprise;

1) by acts of force, occupation, demonstration or gatherings opposes the execution of public works or the demolition of buildings or structures ordered by the administrative or judicial authority;

2) or which have been the subject by the enterprise of an internal or conventional classification in its 24 November 2015 OFFICIAL JOURNAL OF THE TOGOLESE REPUBLIC 77

2) directly or indirectly interrupts or participates directly or indirectly in the interruption of the production or distribution of water, electrical energy, fuels, minerals and raw materials;

If the looted or destroyed foodstuffs or goods are essential or of prime necessity to the economic life of the country, the instigators are liable to a term of criminal imprisonment of five (05) to ten (10) years and a fine of two million (2,000,000) to ten million (10,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

3) willfully obstructs or participates in the obstruction of railways, national roads, port quays;

4) by maneuvers, acts of force, threats or coercion, enters a place assigned to an administrative, scientific or cultural public service, or remains there irregularly and willfully, after having been informed by the authority of the irregular nature of their presence.

###### Art. 635: Any person who, by any means, willfully deteriorates foodstuffs, goods, materials or any instruments used in manufacturing, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of one hundred thousand (100,000) to five hundred thousand (500,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 631: If the offense was committed by an organized gang, the

perpetrators are punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

If the offense is committed by an employee of the enterprise, they are punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s) and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs.

###### Art. 632

: When the total or partial destruction of hydraulic facilities and structures results from an act of vandalism, terrorism or any other reprehensible act knowingly orchestrated and executed, the perpetrators are liable to a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years and a fine of five million (5,000,000) to twenty million (20,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties when this act has resulted in minor material damage.

Section 4: Gambling houses and lotteries, and online gambling or games of chance

###### Art. 636: Any person who, without the required authorization

by law, keeps or has kept for themselves or on behalf of others, a house of games of chance, organizes or operates a lottery or betting games on the result of sporting events or other random events, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs or one of these two penalties.

This penalty may be doubled when the damage results in total decommissioning of the structures in question.

When these reprehensible acts have resulted in serious injuries or loss of human life, the perpetrators are liable to the penalties provided for by this Code for willful injuries and involuntary homicide.

The court seized orders in all cases the confiscation of utensils, apparatus, printed matter or other movable objects used by the guilty parties. It also orders the confiscation of gaming proceeds and the closure of the premises at the time of the finding of the offense, without prejudice to the penalties provided for in Article 54 of this Code in case of conviction of a legal person.

###### Art. 633: The organizers responsible for lotteries and games organized for charitable purposes

referred to in this section are punished with a penalty of five on the occasion of duly authorized events

Art. 664: Constitutes a conspiracy against the internal security of the State, the resolution agreed upon between several persons with a view to committing the acts referred to in the preceding article when this resolution is implemented by one or more material acts tending to the planning or organization of the conspiracy.

Chapter IX: Attacks on public confidence

Section 1: Forgery

Art. 670: Constitutes forgery, any fraudulent alteration of the truth, of a nature to cause harm and accomplished by any means whatsoever, in a document or any other medium for expressing thought which has the object or which may have the effect of establishing proof of a right or of a fact with legal consequences.

The conspiracy is punished by a term of twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment. The penalty is increased to the maximum term of criminal imprisonment when it is committed by a person holding public authority.

###### Paragraph 1

: Forgery in private, commercial or banking documents

###### Art. 665

Any person who publishes, dissemates or reproduces, by any means whatsoever, false news for the purposes referred to in article 688 and any person who refuses to immediately withdraw such information or to make access to it impossible is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s).

Art. 671: Any person who counterfeits, alters or falsifies a deed or document containing acknowledgment, obligation or discharge, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

The author of the false news referred to in the preceding paragraph is punished by a term of imprisonment of three (03) to five (05) years.

If the author of the forgery is a public officer having authority to receive or establish the deed, he is punished by a term of criminal imprisonment of five (05) to ten (10) years.

###### Art. 666 :Any person who commissions or organizes actions aimed at arming or providing weapons to the Togolese population or commissions or organizes actions by an armed gang with the aim of inciting civil war or provoking massacre, is punished by the maximum term of criminal imprisonment.

Art. 672 :Any person who counterfeits, alters or falsifies a banking or commercial document containing acknowledgment, account, obligation or discharge, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of five hundred thousand (500,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

Section 4: Common provisions

If the author of the forgery acted in the exercise of his profession, he is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s).

###### Art. 667 :Any author of a conspiracy aimed at one of the offences referred to in the preceding sections who, before any commencement of execution, reports it to the public authorities, is exempted or dispensed from punishment under the conditions provided for in articles 30 and 31 of this code.

Any author of a conspiracy who, after commencement of execution, enables or facilitates the arrest of his co-authors obtains a mitigating excuse under the conditions provided for in articles 32 to 34 of this code.

The court seized may, in addition, order the disqualification from the right to practice his profession for a period of five (05) years at most.

Art. 673 :Any person who knowingly makes use of forged, altered or falsified deeds, documents or seals, is punished by the same penalties as those applied to the author of the forgery.

Art. 661: Anyone who, having knowledge of acts constituting actions against State security, does not report them immediately to the public authority, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to three (03) year(s).

Art. 674 :Any person who reproduces, alters or falsifies seals, stamps, marks printed or stamped on the head of a person entitled by law to use them, is punished by a term of imprisonment of six (06) months to two (02) years and a fine of two hundred thousand (200,000) to two million (2,000,000) CFA francs.

The court seized shall order the seizure and confiscation of the remuneration received by those guilty of offences against State security, when the remuneration could be seized; its amount is declared acquired to the Public Treasury by the court and deducted as court costs.

Art. 675: Any person who counterfeits or falsifies the seal of the State or of a public administration, marks, hallmarks and other instruments used by public administrations to distinguish acts, documents, materials or objects, is punished by a term of imprisonment of one (01) to five (05) year(s) and a fine of one million (1,000,000) to five million (5,000,000) CFA francs.

82 24 November 2015

Any person who knowingly makes use of counterfeited or falsified seals, marks, hallmarks and other instruments, is punished by the same penalties as those applied to the author of the forgery.

Any person who fraudulently modifies or by any means alters an originally false attestation or certificate.

###### Art. 723: Any person who threatens to commit one of the

1) commits an act of violence against any person on board an aircraft in flight, if such act is likely to compromise the security of the aircraft; or refrains from performing any act is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

2) destroys an aircraft in service or causes to such aircraft damage which renders it incapable of flight or which is likely to compromise its safety in flight;

Paragraph 3: Offenses against the security of a vessel or a fixed platform

3) places or causes to be placed on an aircraft in service, by any means whatsoever, a device or substances likely to destroy the said aircraft or to cause damage to it which renders it incapable of flight or which is likely to compromise its safety in flight;

###### Art. 724: Commits offenses against the security of a

vessel or a fixed platform and is punished by a sentence of five (05) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment if one of the following acts committed compromises or is likely to compromise the security of the vessel or of the fixed platform, any person who:

4) destroys or damages air navigation installations, equipment or services or disrupts their operation, if one of these acts compromises or is likely to compromise the security of an aircraft in flight;

1) commits an act of violence against a person on board a vessel or a fixed platform;

5) communicates information which he or she knows to be false and thereby compromises the security of an aircraft in flight.

2) destroys or causes serious damage to a vessel, its cargo or a fixed platform;

3) places or causes to be placed on a vessel or a fixed platform, by any means whatsoever, a device or substance likely to destroy the vessel or the fixed platform or to cause damage to the vessel, its cargo or the fixed platform;

The expression "aircraft in service", within the meaning of this article, covers the period which extends from the moment when ground personnel or the crew begins to prepare it for a specific flight until the expiration of a period of twenty-four hours following any landing. The period extends in any event to the entire time during which the aircraft is in flight within the meaning of article 719 point 1.

4) destroys or seriously damages maritime navigation installations or services or seriously disrupts their operation;

5) communicates information which he or she knows to be false.

If death of one or more persons has resulted from the acts referred to in this article, the maximum period of imprisonment may be required.

Art. 725: Any person who threatens to commit any one of the offenses provided for in points 1, 2 and 4 of the preceding article in order to compel a natural or legal person to perform or to refrain from performing any act is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10) years of criminal imprisonment.

###### Art. 721: Any person who threatens to commit any one of the offenses provided for in points 2, 3 and 4 of the preceding article aimed at compelling a natural or legal person to perform or to refrain from performing any act is punished by a sentence of five (05) to ten (10)

Art 727: Is punished by a sentence of the maximum period of imprisonment any person who commits against persons enjoying international protection:

1) a weapon or an explosive or incendiary device designed to cause or having the capacity to cause death, serious bodily harm or substantial material damage;

1) a murder, kidnapping or other attack against the person or liberty of a person enjoying international protection;

2) a weapon or device designed to cause death, serious bodily harm or substantial material damage, or which has the capacity to do so, through the emission, dissemination or impact of toxic chemical products, biological agents, toxins or similar substances or radioactive materials.

2) an attack likely to endanger such person by using violence against official premises, private accommodation or means of transport of a person enjoying international protection.

###### Art. 730: For the purposes of this paragraph:

Any person who threatens to commit such an attack is punished by ten (10) to twenty (20) years of criminal imprisonment.

1) the expression "governmental or public facility" means any equipment or means of transport of a permanent or temporary nature which is used or equipped by representatives of the State, members of the Government, Parliament or the Judiciary, or agents or personnel of the State or of any other authority or public entity, or by agents or personnel of an intergovernmental organization, in the context of their official functions;

The expression "person enjoying international protection" means for the purposes of this article:

a) any Head of State, including each member of a collegial body performing under the constitution of the State concerned the functions of Head of State, any Head of Government or any Minister of Foreign Affairs, when such person is in a foreign State, as well as members of his or her family who accompany him or her;

2) the term "infrastructure" means any public or private equipment providing public utility services, such as water supply, sewage disposal, energy, fuel or communications;

b) any representative, official or official personality of the State or other agent of an intergovernmental organization who, at the date and place where an offense is committed against his or her person, official premises, private residence or means of transport, is entitled under international law to special protection against any attack on his or her person, liberty or dignity, as well as members of his or her family who are part of his or her household.

3) the expression "public place" means parts of any building, land, public thoroughfare, waterway and other place which are accessible to the public, continuously, periodically or occasionally and includes any place for commercial, cultural, historical, educational, religious, official, entertainment, recreational or other use which is thus accessible or open to the public;

4) the expression "public transport system" means all equipment, vehicles and means, public or private, which are used in the context of services for the transport of persons or goods accessible to the public.

3)carry out water intakes, placed on the public domain developments, apparatus or installations, or dug a well with a view to carrying out water intakes, without the authorization provided for by the environment code or outside the limits and conditions thereof;

If the offense was committed in the context of the activity of a legal entity, that legal entity and its officers are criminally liable. The legal entity is punished by the penalties provided for by article 54 of this code.

###### Art. 902: Physical persons guilty of the infractions

CFA when carried out under conditions defined in articles 98 and above may also commit the following offenses which appear to have no economic justification or purpose:

g) manual exchange operations subject to a regulatory limit, likely to be carried out for the purposes of money laundering, when their amount reaches five million (5,000,000) CFA francs;

h) contribution or exchange of chips or gaming plaques, in a casino or any other gaming establishment, for an amount greater than or equal to one million (1,000,000) CFA francs or in cash or equivalent value greater than or equal to that amount;

These persons informed by all means or the persons referred to by law who remain silent on acts, objects, materials and the violation of permits or licenses that they have falsified or forged.

Persons other than those provided for by the law who are certified and use payment cards during five (5) to ten (10) years;

The prohibition to hold and carry a weapon subject to authorization during five (5) to ten (10) years;

The confiscation of any property, object or proceeds that was intended for, used in or resulting from the money laundering offense.

The persons may be punished with the following additional penalties:

1) definitive prohibition from the national territory or for a duration of one (01) to five (05) year(s) against any foreigner convicted;

2) prohibition from leaving the national territory and withdrawal of passport for a duration of one (01) to three (03) year(s) in certain prefectures of the country;

3) prohibition from leaving the national territory and withdrawal of passport for a duration of one (01) to three (03) years;

4) prohibition of civil and political rights for a duration of one (01) to three (03) year(s);

5) prohibition to engage in the profession or activity on the occasion of which the infraction was committed and prohibition to exercise a public function;

6) prohibition, definitive or for another duration other than that which permits the withdrawal of hunting or fishing license by the offender or holder of the license or permit, or the prohibition and use of payment cards during five (05) to ten (10) years;

7) prohibition to carry and possession of weapons subject to authorization during five (05) to ten (10) years;

8) prohibition to issue checks of certain origins and subject to the declaration conditions provided by the law, which directly lead to the disclosure of amounts, money or origin from the proceeds of any object susceptible of restitution.

4) informed by all means or the persons referred to by law who have been prosecuted for money laundering offenses of which they had knowledge by reason of their profession or their functions, and which no infraction has been committed;

5) communicated to the judicial authorities or to the competent officials to establish the infractions of origin and subsequent acts as provided by law when they have falsified or forged;

6) communicated false information or agreements regarding persons other than those provided by the legislative provisions relating to identification obligations in matters of combating money laundering;

The declaration conditions and transfers or documents provided by law, which directly lead to the disclosure of amounts, money or origin from the proceeds, the execution of the object susceptible of restitution.

###### Art.903

Legal persons other than the State, on whose account or for whose benefit a money laundering offense or one of the infractions provided by the present section has been committed by one of its organs or representatives, are punished with one of the penalties provided by article 54 of the present code, without prejudice to the conviction of physical persons as authors or accomplices of the same acts.

Legal persons, other than the State, may, in addition, be sentenced to one or more of the following penalties:

1) Those provided by article 54 of the present code;

2) placement under judicial supervision for a duration of five (05) years at most;

3) prohibition, definitively, or for a duration of five (05) to ten (10) years at most, from exercising directly or indirectly one or more professional or social activities on the occasion of which the infraction was committed;

4) posting of the pronounced decision or dissemination of it by the written press or by any means of audiovisual communication, at the expense of the convicted legal person, in accordance with the provisions of article 142 of the present code.

The sanctions provided in points 2, 3, 4 of the preceding paragraph, the definitive closure of an establishment and the dissolution of the legal person are not applicable to financial institutions under a supervisory authority with disciplinary power.

The competent supervisory authority, referred by the public prosecutor of any prosecution initiated against a financial institution, may take appropriate sanctions, in accordance with the specific legislative and regulatory texts in force.

The penalties for the latter are reduced by half. In addition, it is exempt from the fine and, where applicable, from accessory measures and optional supplementary penalties.

Art. 906: In all cases of conviction for a money laundering offense or attempt, the courts order the confiscation for the benefit of the public treasury of the proceeds derived from the offense, of movable or immovable property into which these proceeds are transformed or converted and up to the amount of their value, and property acquired legitimately to which said proceeds are mixed, as well as income and other advantages derived from these proceeds, property into which they are transformed or invested or property to which they are mixed, to whomever these proceeds and property belong, unless their owner establishes that he was unaware of their fraudulent origin at the time of the facts.

###### Art. 904 ;

Any person guilty, on the one hand, of participation in an association or agreement, with a view to committing one of the infractions provided in articles 898 to 902 above and, on the other hand, of aid, incitement or advice to a physical or legal person, with a view to executing them or facilitating their execution, is exempt from criminal sanctions if, having revealed the existence of this agreement, association, aid or advice to the judicial authority, it allows thus, on the one hand to identify the other persons involved and, on the other hand, to prevent the realization of the infraction.

###### Art. 907; Individuals prosecuted for the infractions referred to in the present chapter are subject to extradition whatever the duration of sentence incurred on the national territory; individuals who, for infractions referred to in the present chapter, are definitively convicted by the courts of the requesting State without it being necessary to take into account the sentence pronounced.

There is no derogation from the common law rules of extradition, in particular those relating to double incrimination.
